=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import java.io.*;
import java.util.*;

public class CSVProcessor {

    public void readCSV(String fileName, List<String> title, List<List<String>> data) throws IOException {
        try (BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(fileName))) {
            String line = reader.readLine();
            if (line != null) {
                title.addAll(Arrays.asList(line.split(",")));

                String lineData;
                while ((lineData = reader.readLine()) != null) {
                    data.add(Arrays.asList(lineData.split(",")));
                }
            }
        }
    }

    public int writeCSV(List<String> title, List<List<String>> data, String fileName) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(fileName))) {
            writer.write(String.join(",", title));
            writer.newLine();

            for (List<String> row : data) {
                writer.write(String.join(",", row));
                writer.newLine();
            }
            return 1;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return 0;
        }
    }

    public int writeCSV(List<List<String>> data, String fileName) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(fileName))) {
            for (List<String> row : data) {
                writer.write(String.join(",", row));
                writer.newLine();
            }
            return 1;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return 0;
        }
    }

    public int processCSVData(int N, String saveFileName) throws IOException {
        List<String> title = new ArrayList<>();
        List<List<String>> data = new ArrayList<>();
        readCSV(saveFileName, title, data);

        List<String> columnData = new ArrayList<>();
        for (List<String> row : data) {
            if (N < row.size()) {
                columnData.add(row.get(N).toUpperCase());
            }
        }

        List<List<String>> newData = new ArrayList<>();
        newData.add(columnData);

        return writeCSV(title, newData, saveFileName.split("\\.")[0] + "_process.csv");
    }
}


=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to py.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import java.io.*;
import java.util.*;

public class CSVProcessor {

    public void readCSV(String fileName, List<String> title, List<List<String>> data) throws IOException {
        try (BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(fileName))) {
            String line = reader.readLine();
            if (line != null) {
                title.addAll(Arrays.asList(line.split(",")));

                String lineData;
                while ((lineData = reader.readLine()) != null) {
                    data.add(Arrays.asList(lineData.split(",")));
                }
            }
        }
    }

    public int writeCSV(List<String> title, List<List<String>> data, String fileName) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(fileName))) {
            writer.write(String.join(",", title));
            writer.newLine();

            for (List<String> row : data) {
                writer.write(String.join(",", row));
                writer.newLine();
            }
            return 1;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return 0;
        }
    }

    public int writeCSV(List<List<String>> data, String fileName) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(fileName))) {
            for (List<String> row : data) {
                writer.write(String.join(",", row));
                writer.newLine();
            }
            return 1;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return 0;
        }
    }

    public int processCSVData(int N, String saveFileName) throws IOException {
        List<String> title = new ArrayList<>();
        List<List<String>> data = new ArrayList<>();
        readCSV(saveFileName, title, data);

        List<String> columnData = new ArrayList<>();
        for (List<String> row : data) {
            if (N < row.size()) {
                columnData.add(row.get(N).toUpperCase());
            }
        }

        List<List<String>> newData = new ArrayList<>();
        newData.add(columnData);

        return writeCSV(title, newData, saveFileName.split("\\.")[0] + "_process.csv");
    }
}

```

Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior.

Keep the reasoning compact and single-pass:
- Identify only the pieces that must remain equivalent: signatures, I/O contract, stateful behavior, control flow, and key API semantics.
- Turn that mapping into target-language structure promptly.
- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.
- If a mismatch appears, patch only the affected local section and recheck once.
- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

Output only the translated py code in a code block.

```
public String[] split(CharSequence input, int limit) {
    int index = 0;
    boolean matchLimited = limit > 0;
    ArrayList<String> matchList = new ArrayList<>();
    Matcher m = matcher(input);
    // Add segments before each match found
    while(m.find()) {
        if (!matchLimited || matchList.size() < limit - 1) {
            if (index == 0 && index == m.start() && m.start() == m.end()) {
                // no empty leading substring included for zero-width match at the beginning
                continue;
            }
            String match = input.subSequence(index, m.start()).toString();
            matchList.add(match);
            index = m.end();
        } else if (matchList.size() == limit - 1) { // last one
            String match = input.subSequence(index, input.length()).toString();
            matchList.add(match);
            index = m.end();
        }
    }
    // If no match was found, return this
    if (index == 0)
        return new String[] {input.toString()};
    // Add remaining segment
    if (!matchLimited || matchList.size() < limit)
        matchList.add(input.subSequence(index, input.length()).toString());
    // Construct result
    int resultSize = matchList.size();
    if (limit == 0) {
        while (resultSize > 0 && matchList.get(resultSize-1).equals(""))
            resultSize--;
    }
    String[] result = new String[resultSize];
    return matchList.subList(0, resultSize).toArray(result);
}
```
There is a special case: `if (index == 0) return new String[] {input.toString()};` after the loop. This means if no match found, it returns the original input as a single-element array, BEFORE trailing empty string removal. So `"".split(",")` returns `[""]`. So ",".split(",") returns empty array. Good.

So helper: 
```
def java_split(s):
    parts = s.split(",")
    if not s:
        return [""]
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts
```
But careful: if s is empty, s.split(",") returns [""]; if we then while parts and parts[-1] == "", we'd pop and return [], which is wrong. So need special case: if s == "" return [""]. Or use the logic from Java: if no comma in s, return [s]. Actually Java's split returns original if no match found, including empty. So:
```
def java_split(s):
    if "," not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(",")
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts
```
This matches: if no comma, return [s] (including empty). If comma present, split and remove trailing empties. For ",", parts ["",""] -> pop both -> []. For "a,", ["a",""] -> pop -> ["a"]. For "a,,b", ["a","","b"] -> no trailing empty -> ["a","","b"]. Good. This is simpler and exactly matches Java's behavior? Let's verify with "a,": comma present, parts ["a",""], pop -> ["a"]. So if called with 2 args, it's data, fileName. If called with 3 args, it's title, data, fileName. We can implement:
```
def writeCSV(self, *args):
    if len(args) == 2:
        data, fileName = args
        title = None
    else:
        title, data, fileName = args
    try:
        with open(fileName, 'w') as writer:
            if title is not None:
                writer.write(','.join(title) + '\n')
            for row in data:
                writer.write(','.join(row) + '\n')
        return 1
    except OSError:
        return 0
```
This preserves behavior. But note: In Java, if title is null? Not likely. We can use `title is not None`.

Now, we need to output only the translated py code in a code block. We'll include the helper function inside class or module-level. Since Java class has methods, we can define class CSVProcessor with methods. Helper can be a static method or nested function. To keep behavior identical, we can define a module-level function `_java_split` or a static method. But the translated code should be self-contained. We'll define `_java_split` at module level.

Let's write code:

```
def _java_split(s):
    if "," not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(",")
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts

class CSVProcessor:
    def readCSV(self, fileName, title, data):
        with open(fileName, 'r') as reader:
            line = reader.readline()
            if line != '':
                title.extend(_java_split(line.rstrip('\n')))
                for lineData in reader:
                    data.append(_java_split(lineData.rstrip('\n')))

    def writeCSV(self, *args):
        if len(args) == 2:
            data, fileName = args
            title = None
        else:
            title, data, fileName = args
        try:
            with open(fileName, 'w') as writer:
                if title is not None:
                    writer.write(','.join(title) + '\n')
                for row in data:
                    writer.write(','.join(row) + '\n')
            return 1
        except OSError:
            return 0

    def processCSVData(self, N, saveFileName):
        title = []
        data = []
        self.readCSV(saveFileName, title, data)
        columnData = []
        for row in data:
            if N < len(row):
                columnData.append(row[N].upper())
        newData = [columnData]
        return self.writeCSV(title, newData, saveFileName.split(".")[0] + "_process.csv")
```

Need to verify `readCSV` line handling: If file has a line with no newline at EOF, `readline()` returns "abc" (no newline), `rstrip('\n')` returns "abc". Good. If file has an empty line at EOF? Actually if file ends with newline, after reading all lines, `readline()` returns '' at EOF. Good.

```
def _java_split(s, delim):
    if delim not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(delim)
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts
```

Then for comma: `_java_split(line, ",")`. For dot: `_java_split(saveFileName, ".")[0]`. This will match Java's behavior for literal delimiters. Note: Java's split uses regex, but for comma and dot (escaped) it's literal. So generic helper works.

Now, let's consider if `_java_split` should be defined as:
```
def _java_split(s, delim):
    if delim not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(delim)
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts
```
This is correct.

Now, let's consider if `_java_split` should handle the case where `s` is empty and `delim` is in `s`? Impossible because empty string has no characters. So `delim not in s` true. Good.

Now, let's consider if `_java_split` should handle the case where `s` is a string and `delim` is a backslash? Not used.

Now, let's consider if `_java_split` should handle the case where `s` is a string and `delim` is a newline? Not used.

Now, let's consider if `_java_split` should handle the case where `s` is a string and `delim` is a comma and `s` is ",". We already did.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
, the output must be only the translated py code in a code block. We have already written the code. Let's produce final code block.

But wait, we need to ensure the code is correct and complete. Let's review final code:

```
def _java_split(s, delim):
    if delim not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(delim)
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts

class CSVProcessor:
    def readCSV(self, fileName, title, data):
        with open(fileName, 'r', errors='replace') as reader:
            line = reader.readline()
            if line != '':
                title.extend(_java_split(line.rstrip('\n'), ','))
                for lineData in reader:
                    data.append(_java_split(lineData.rstrip('\n'), ','))

    def writeCSV(self, *args):
        if len(args) == 2:
            data, fileName = args
            title = None
        else:
            title, data, fileName = args
        try:
            with open(fileName, 'w', errors='replace') as writer:
                if title is not None:
                    writer.write(','.join(title) + '\n')
                for row in data:
                    writer.write(','.join(row) + '\n')
            return 1
        except OSError:
            return 0

    def processCSVData(self, N, saveFileName):
        title = []
        data = []
        self.readCSV(saveFileName, title, data)
        columnData = []
        for row in data:
            if N < len(row):
                if N < 0:
                    raise IndexError("Index out of range")
                columnData.append(row[N].upper())
        newData = [columnData]
        return self.writeCSV(title, newData, _java_split(saveFileName, ".")[0] + "_process.csv")
```

Need to consider: In `readCSV`, if the first line is empty string at EOF? We check `line != ''`. If file is empty, line is '', no changes. If file has a first line that is empty (just newline), line is '\n', not '', so title gets [""]. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: `line.rstrip('\n')` on a line that is just "\n" returns "". Then `_java_split("", ",")` returns [""]. Good.

Now, what about a line that is "abc\n" and we rstrip -> "abc". Good.

Now, what about a line that is "abc\r\n" and universal newline returns "abc\n", rstrip -> "abc". Good.

Now, what about a line that is "abc\r" and universal newline returns "abc\n", rstrip -> "abc". Good.

Now, what about a line that is "abc" at EOF, rstrip -> "abc". Good.

Now, what about a line that is "\n" (empty line) and we rstrip -> "", `_java_split("", ",")` -> [""]. Good.

Now, what about a line that is "\r\n" (empty line) and universal newline returns "\n", rstrip -> "", same.

Now, what about a line that is "\r" (empty line) and universal newline returns "\n", rstrip -> "", same.

Now, what about a line that is "abc\n\n"? This is two lines, not one. So no issue.

Now, what about a file with no newline at EOF and last line is empty? Not possible; empty file is no lines.

Now, let's consider if `readCSV` should use `line.rstrip('\n')` and if the line is "abc\n" and there are more lines, for loop starts at next line. Good.

=== TRANSLATED CODE (py) ===
def _java_split(s, delim):
    if delim not in s:
        return [s]
    parts = s.split(delim)
    while parts and parts[-1] == "":
        parts.pop()
    return parts

class CSVProcessor:
    def readCSV(self, fileName, title, data):
        with open(fileName, 'r', errors='replace') as reader:
            line = reader.readline()
            if line != '':
                title.extend(_java_split(line.rstrip('\n'), ','))
                for lineData in reader:
                    data.append(_java_split(lineData.rstrip('\n'), ','))

    def writeCSV(self, *args):
        if len(args) == 2:
            data, fileName = args
            title = None
        else:
            title, data, fileName = args
        try:
            with open(fileName, 'w', errors='replace') as writer:
                if title is not None:
                    writer.write(','.join(title) + '\n')
                for row in data:
                    writer.write(','.join(row) + '\n')
            return 1
        except OSError:
            return 0

    def processCSVData(self, N, saveFileName):
        title = []
        data = []
        self.readCSV(saveFileName, title, data)
        columnData = []
        for row in data:
            if N < len(row):
                if N < 0:
                    raise IndexError("Index out of range")
                columnData.append(row[N].upper())
        newData = [columnData]
        return self.writeCSV(title, newData, _java_split(saveFileName, ".")[0] + "_process.csv")
